\title[Attainable information geometry]{Attainable information geometry\\ in positive experiments}
\author{Qian Qi}
\date{September 7, 2026}
\subjclass[2020]{62B05, 62C10, 93E11, 68Q19, 13D40}

exponent collisions, moment pairings, finite-state filtering}
\hypersetup{pdftitle={Attainable information geometry in positive experiments},pdfauthor={Qian Qi}}
\begin{document}
\begin{abstract}
We determine the sharp finite-memory prediction law for positive sparse
monomial experiments at a fixed horizon, uniformly through all additive
exponent collisions. Maximal Vandermonde products of future exponents,
truncated by the dimension acquired by the past, determine both
checkpoint error and the error of one causal finite-state filter.
A general transfer theorem separates the required whole-image geometry,
unconditional acquisition mass, and stable reachable-state updates; it
also gives the accumulated error for unequal stage budgets. The monomial
verification uses complete acquired Newton--Hermite flags and raw-moment
updates without reciprocal collision gaps. A second multistep theorem
for bounded finite observation algebras identifies the covariance
exterior-power profile uniformly over all priors and all rank losses;
its proof verifies the same transfer hypotheses by exact product
coordinates and an unconditional density argument. Complete companion results
treat command-polynomial rank strata on compact latent spaces, the
uniform one-step covariance profile, exact kernels of continuous moment
pairings, circular contrast, operational reconstruction of the resolution
profile, and prior ambiguity. The kernel criterion combines dimension-free
full-support feasibility with the rank of a quotient multiplication
pencil and supplies arbitrarily small bounded-density witnesses.
\end{abstract}
\maketitle
\section{Introduction}
A posterior distribution contains more information than is needed for a
finite future experiment. The amount that can be discarded depends on
which posterior distributions the past can produce, which functions the
future can test, and whether a compressed state can be updated after the
next report. At a degeneracy these three questions need not have the
same answer. An observation matrix may lose a direction which the past
has scarcely acquired; a regular local image need not control all
histories; and a sequence of checkpoint encoders need not be a causal
filter.

We determine their joint effect for positive sparse monomial experiments.
The main invariant is a finite list of maximal Vandermonde products of
future exponents, truncated by the dimension acquired by the past. It
gives the sharp prediction error for every integer memory budget,
uniformly through all additive collisions, and the same law is achieved
by one causal filter. Thus the result is not merely an exponent at a
fixed nonsingular parameter: it determines the transition between
memory regimes as directions become indistinguishable.

The proof proceeds by a transfer principle formulated for reachable
sets, actual acquisition measures, and state updates. Its geometric
hypotheses are a dimension-truncated whole-image cover and positive
mass in every initial resolution flag. A uniform update bound then
turns all checkpoint covers into a single causal realization, with an
explicit error recurrence for unequal stage budgets. The geometric
verification for monomial experiments is the main model-specific
argument. It pairs a product tangent generated by real commands with
complete Newton--Hermite prefixes and retains the probability of the
report word throughout.

\subsection{The main theorem}
Let
\[
 A=\{0=a_0<a_1<\cdots<a_{r-1}\},\qquad
 W_A=\operatorname{span}\{t^{a_i}:0\le i<r\},\qquad r\ge2.
\]
Throughout the monomial part we put
\[
 I=[l,u]=[0,1],\qquad D=a_{r-1},\qquad
 mA=\{a_{i_1}+\cdots+a_{i_m}:0\le i_j<r\},\qquad h_A(m)=|mA|,
\]
with $0A=\{0\}$ and $h_A(0)=1$. These are sets of distinct sums;
formal labels used below retain their multiplicities.
The latent variable lies in $I$ and has a fixed full-support
probability $\mu$, not necessarily admitting a density. A fixed matrix
$C=(c_{ji})$ of column rank $r$ specifies a finite detector
\[
 k_j^a(t)=\sum_i c_{ji}t^{a_i},\qquad
 \sum_j k_j^a(t)=1,\qquad k_j^a(t)\ge k_*>0.
\]
The known exponent vector $a$ ranges over a compact subset $K$ of the
strictly ordered one-step chamber, and these inequalities hold on $K$.
A command $g\in[\eta,1-\eta]^J$, $0<\eta<1/2$, retains cell $j$
with probability $g_j$. The reports are the retained cell or failure;
all trials, including failures, are counted. Every report likelihood is
bounded below by a fixed $\kappa>0$. Acquisition commands are independent
and uniform for the unconditional criterion.

Fix a total horizon $N\ge2$ and choose
$H\ge\max\{1,N\max_K a_{r-1}\}$. At checkpoint $n$ there are
$m=N-n$ future trials. Keep every formal label
\[
 \Lambda_m^+=\{\alpha\in\N_0^r:|\alpha|=m\}\setminus\{me_0\},
 \qquad q_m=\binom{m+r-1}{r-1}-1,
 \qquad x_\alpha(a)=H^{-1}\alpha\cdot a,
\]
even when several labels have equal values. For $1\le\ell\le q_m$ put
\[
 \mathcal V_{m,\ell}(a)=\max_{\substack{F\subset\Lambda_m^+\\|F|=\ell}}
     \prod_{\{\alpha,\beta\}\subset F}|x_\alpha(a)-x_\beta(a)|,
 \qquad \mathcal V_{m,1}=1.
\]
A zero product is retained. With $p_{n,m}=\min\{n(r-1),q_m\}$ define
\[
 \Psi_{n,m}(M,a)=\max_{1\le\ell\le p_{n,m}}
       (\mathcal V_{m,\ell}(a)/M)^{2/\ell},\qquad
 \Xi_N(M,a)=\max_{1\le n<N}\Psi_{n,N-n}(M,a).
\]
Endpoint profiles are zero. The future query is sampled independently
\emph{after} the memory label is formed, from a fixed finite spanning
menu of actual $m$-trial products. Only that query is executed. Regret
is excess squared prediction loss for its binary outcome. A checkpoint
encoder can read the complete prefix before keeping one of $M$ labels;
a causal filter updates only its retained label using the current
command and report. Definition~\ref{def:finite-state} gives the precise
resource and loss conventions.

\begin{theorem}[Intrinsic attainable resolution and causal memory]\label{thm:resolution-main}
Under these hypotheses there are constants $0<c\le C<\infty$,
depending only on the fixed experiment, $K$, $N$ and $\mu$, with the
following properties for every known calibration $a\in K$ and every
integer $M\ge1$.

At each checkpoint $n+m=N$, the optimal $M$-message squared-prediction
regret is between $c\Psi_{n,m}(M,a)$ and $C\Psi_{n,m}(M,a)$,
both unconditionally under independent uniform acquisition commands and
in the minimax sense over admitted histories. The query is sampled
independently after the index is formed, from the ordered products of
one fixed attainable failure-factor basis; all its $m$ trials are counted.

There exists one deterministic stage-compatible transducer, allowed to
depend on $a$ and $M$, storing at most $M$ persistent labels after
every report, whose regret at every checkpoint and on every history is
at most $C\Xi_N(M,a)$. Conversely every such transducer has maximum
unconditional checkpoint regret at least $c\Xi_N(M,a)$ under the
same common exploration law. Independent coding randomization does not
remove the lower bound. The clock and fixed real-valued program are
read-only as in Definition~\ref{def:finite-state}.

The constants are uniform across all additive collision strata of $K$.
No calibration-blind codebook or uniformity over all full-support priors
is part of the assertion.
\end{theorem}


At a fixed calibration the largest nonzero index is
$\min\{n(r-1),|mA|-1\}$, the exact continuous-state dimension of
Theorem~\ref{thm:main}. Away from a collision this dimension determines
the small-error exponent. The full list $\mathcal V_{m,\ell}$ also
determines when that exponent becomes visible. In particular,
Corollary~\ref{cor:intrinsic-bits} recovers the persistent-bit law, and
Theorem~\ref{thm:collision-tree} gives the phases along collision paths
of arbitrary finite contact orders. Intersecting collision loci need
not reduce to one gap; the two-parameter example in
Section~\ref{sec:collision-geometry} gives distinct intermediate regimes.

\subsection{The implication from geometry to causality}
The transfer theorem, Theorem~\ref{thm:causal-transfer}, is stated for
an arbitrary family of compact reachable states with linear physical
query coordinates. After a uniformly invertible coordinate change,
suppose the entire state set has bounded semialgebraic format,
dimension at most $p_n$, and containing side lengths
$s_{n,1}\ge\cdots\ge s_{n,q_n}\ge0$. Suppose also that one common
exploration experiment supplies positive unconditional mass on a
rectangle in every initial nonzero flag of length at most $p_n$.
If the exact updates are uniformly Lipschitz and preserve reachability,
then the checkpoint error is
\[
 Q_n(M)=\max_{\ell\le p_n}
       \left(\frac{s_{n,1}\cdots s_{n,\ell}}{M}\right)^{2/\ell}.
\]
For arbitrary stage budgets $M_n$, one causal filter has error at most
a fixed-horizon multiple of $\max_n Q_n(M_n)$, and every filter has
error at least another such multiple under that same exploration law.
The theorem gives the full convolution of the preceding quantization
errors, rather than replacing the accumulated error by the last one.

This implication is useful precisely when well-conditioned geometric
coordinates have no well-conditioned recursion. In the monomial
problem, divided differences describe the resolution flags, but raw
remaining moments carry the filter. Positivity of a report likelihood
bounds the Bayes denominator along every segment between reachable
moment states. Lemma~\ref{lem:positive-remaining-update} supplies the
update bound without a reciprocal collision gap, on arbitrary compact
latent spaces with finite remaining-test closure. It does not assert
that every such experiment has the same attainable geometry.

The model-specific verification has three steps. First, the binomial
product tangent in Lemma~\ref{lem:binomial-tangent} has dimension
$n(r-1)+1$. Pairing it with a complete confluent prefix and removing
evidence loses exactly one direction. The inverse chart includes
kernel coordinates; integrating them while retaining the all-failure
probability proves Lemma~\ref{lem:newton-attainment}. Second, each
report-word image is rational in its command variables. Normalizing
each factor gives dimension at most $n(r-1)$, and the
thin-rectangle estimate of Lemma~\ref{lem:tame-rectangle} covers its
\emph{whole} image, not a section. Third, the remaining-test update
lemma supplies the causal hypothesis. Section~\ref{sec:transfer-application}
checks these hypotheses explicitly, including a bounded invertible
extension of the Newton coordinate change at exact collisions, and
proves Theorem~\ref{thm:resolution-main}.

The elementary cover-to-filter argument is not the source of a new
entropy inequality or a new interpolation identity. The substantive
compatibility is that all acquired flags needed for the lower bound,
the dimensional truncation of the global upper bound, and one stable
recursion remain valid at the same degeneracies. Without the acquired
measure one obtains only an observation-side estimate; without the
global cover one controls only selected histories; without the update
one obtains checkpoint encoders rather than a filter.

\subsection{A second multistep classification}
The transfer theorem also yields a covariance law for a different
experimental class. Let $f_1,\ldots,f_d$ be uniformly bounded continuous
functions on a compact space, and suppose $\operatorname{span}\{1,f_i\}$
is closed under multiplication with uniformly bounded coefficient
representations. No independence of these functions is imposed. With
commands $u\in[-\delta,\delta]^d$, where $d\delta\le1/4$ after
normalizing $|f_i|\le1$, use the binary likelihoods
$L_y(u,x)=(1+y\,u^tf(x))/2$. For any prior $\mu$, write
\[
 \Sigma_\mu=\Cov_\mu(f,f),\qquad
 \mathcal E_{\Sigma_\mu}(M)=
 \max_{1\le\ell\le d}
 \left(\frac{\|\bigwedge^\ell\Sigma_\mu\|_{\rm op}}{M}\right)^{2/\ell}.
\]
Theorem~\ref{thm:algebra-multistep} proves that this is the sharp
checkpoint and causal prediction profile at every fixed positive
checkpoint, using a specified spanning menu of actual future products.
The constants are uniform over \emph{all} priors and over the stated
families of functions, including simultaneous support and covariance
rank losses. Unequal stage budgets satisfy the same causal law with
$\max_n\mathcal E_{\Sigma_\mu}(M_n)$.

This is not a deduction from the rank of a moment matrix alone.
Multiplication closure bounds the coefficients of every acquired
likelihood product. Its prediction state is exactly
$\mu f+\Sigma_\mu\theta$, with $\theta$ uniformly bounded over
\emph{all} histories. A positive-volume family of genuine prefixes
supplies a uniform ball density in $\theta$ at each checkpoint.
Raw moments, rather than inverse-covariance coordinates, give the
update. These three facts verify the transfer hypotheses independently
of the size of any covariance eigenvalue. The proof is in
Section~\ref{sec:algebra-multistep}.

The two multistep laws have different sources of geometry. Sparse
monomial products need increasing test spaces and acquired
Newton--Hermite flags; a bounded observation algebra has a fixed
product space and a covariance ellipsoid. Finite-dimensional real
function algebras factor through a finite observable partition, as
proved in Proposition~\ref{prop:finite-observable-quotient}. Thus the
second theorem does not replace the interval theorem or assert closure
for arbitrary continuous detectors. It supplies a separate,
prior-uniform multistep use of the same transfer principle.

\subsection{Companion results and the organization of the proofs}
The principal proof is contained in Sections~\ref{sec:experiment}--\ref{sec:transfer-application},
with the bit and collision-path consequences immediately following it.
The appendices are organized by mathematical dependence and contain the
complete companion arguments, not just statements or proof sketches.
Appendix~\ref{app:structural} classifies the fixed-prior dimension
exponents of command-polynomial experiments and proves the uniform
one-step covariance profile. Appendix~\ref{app:pairings} treats
full-support moment pairings: a positivity criterion gives annihilation,
whereas the rank of a quotient multiplication pencil characterizes an
\emph{exact} kernel. Its square moving-kernel example separates this
criterion from feasibility, closure and dimension counting. The finite
unconstrained maximum-rank case is the classical linear
matroid-intersection calculation; the constrained compact-space result
uses an open chart of bounded density perturbations instead.

Appendix~\ref{app:additional-geometry} contains the circular-contrast
law, the operational inverse of the budget profile, and the joint
memory--prior-ambiguity geometry. Appendix~\ref{app:complete-routes}
contains the complete direct proofs of
Theorems~\ref{thm:intrinsic-checkpoint} and
\ref{thm:intrinsic-streaming}, together with their specialized analytic routes.
Appendix~\ref{app:decisions} records the observation algebra and decision
consequences. Appendix~\ref{app:effective} supplies the finite numerical
realization, resource and stability results. Precise comparisons with
classical inputs and neighboring approximation problems are collected
in Appendix~\ref{app:comparison}.

The scope distinctions matter. A rank formula for a specified prior
is not a multiscale formula uniform through rank loss. A local
exact-kernel theorem is not a global causal classification. The
different geometric classes and the uncertainty statements retain their
own assumptions, summarized in Table~\ref{tab:scope}. In particular,
the main memory resource counts persistent labels, not every bit of
the fixed program or arithmetic workspace. Its constants are uniform
in the calibration and integer budget at the stated fixed horizon,
not uniformly over all full-support priors or unbounded horizons.
The observation-algebra theorem has the additional prior uniformity
stated above; it retains the fixed-horizon convention.

\section{Calibrated experiments and their operational quotient}\label{sec:experiment}
All spaces carry their Borel sigma-algebras. Deterministic continuation,
transition and decision rules are Borel measurable; randomized rules are
Borel probability kernels. Every external random seed is independent of
the hidden parameter and the acquired history unless explicitly charged
as part of that history.

Let $k_1,\ldots,k_K\in W_A$ be strictly positive on $I$, satisfy
$\sum_jk_j=1$, and span $W_A$. On each trial a fresh raw symbol has these
probabilities conditional on the same fixed parameter $t$. The raw trials
are conditionally independent. A command
$g\in\cG=[\eta,1-\eta]^K$ accepts raw symbol $j$ with probability $g_j$,
using an independent prepared uniform variable. A rejected symbol is
reported as $\dagger$. Thus the report alphabet is
$\cX=\{1,\ldots,K,\dagger\}$ and its likelihood factors are
\begin{equation}\label{eq:channel}
 f_{g,j}=g_jk_j,\qquad
 f_{g,\dagger}=F_g=\sum_j(1-g_j)k_j.
\end{equation}
A single command is applied at every possible parameter value. Every
trial is counted, including every rejected report. There is a constant
$\kappa>0$ such that $\kappa\le f_{g,x}(t)\le1$ for all $g,x,t$.
Indeed one may take $\kappa=\eta\min_{j,t}k_j(t)$.

\begin{lemma}[Attainable interior and realizability]\label{lem:interior}
The failure family is precisely the image of the rejection cube under
$u\mapsto\sum_ju_jk_j$. It contains a neighborhood of the constant $1/2$
in $W_A$. Conversely, every finite exponent set $A$ as above has a positive
normalized $r$-cell realization with this property.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The displayed map is a surjective linear map onto $W_A$. Choose a linear
right inverse $L:W_A\to\R^K$. For every $v$ satisfying
$\norm{Lv}_\infty<1/2-\eta$, rejection vector
$u=\one/2+Lv$ realizes $1/2+v$ inside the cube. This proves both the
interior statement and that the same physical command implements it.
For existence, choose $\delta>0$ with
$\delta\sum_{a\in A\setminus\{0\}}u^a<1/r$ and set
\[
 k_a(t)=1/r+\delta t^a\quad(a\ne0),\qquad
 k_0(t)=1/r-\delta\sum_{a\ne0}t^a.
\]
They are positive and sum to one. Their span contains one and then each
$t^a=(k_a-1/r)/\delta$, so has dimension $r$. These are calibrated
categorical experiments. No mechanical origin is asserted for this general
realization; the separate Lorentz realization is derived in
Section~\ref{sec:common-risk}.
\end{proof}

A history $h=(g_1,x_1,\ldots,g_n,x_n)$ has posterior
\begin{equation}\label{eq:posterior}
 P_h(t)\mu(\dd t),\qquad
 P_h=\frac{\prod_{i=1}^nf_{g_i,x_i}}{\mu(\prod_{i=1}^nf_{g_i,x_i})}.
\end{equation}
All commands are known inputs when received. They cease to be available
once discarded by a memory-limited filter. A continuous history encoding
means continuity in $(g_1,\ldots,g_n)$ separately for each fixed report
word. It need not be injective on histories and need not identify all
histories with the same posterior.

\begin{definition}[Common future-only continuation]\label{def:future}
At a checkpoint a continuation has the same fixed task data in the two
experiments. Its commands are functions only of the reports and commands
\emph{since that checkpoint}, and any fresh independent random seed. Its
first command is the same in both experiments. It may not read the
original prefix, a prefix-dependent seed, or any other uncharged historical
tape. Two posteriors are $m$-equivalent when all such length-$m$
continuations have the same future report law.
\end{definition}

A separate payoff automaton referring to past observations is task memory,
not something removed by posterior equivalence. Losses that inspect $t$
directly are also not included without adding their test functions. This
quantifier matters: two different constant failure commands leave the
same prior, but a rule that reads and repeats the original command does
not meet Definition~\ref{def:future}.

\begin{proposition}[Exact future test space]\label{prop:tests}
The linear span of length-$m$ future likelihood tests is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:future-space}
 W_A^m=\spanop\{t^s:s\in mA\}.
\end{equation}
Two posteriors are $m$-equivalent if and only if their moments at the
exponents in $mA\setminus\{0\}$ agree. A finite list of actually
executable, nonadaptive all-failure probes spans the same space.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Choose finitely many attainable failures spanning $W_A$, using
Lemma~\ref{lem:interior}. All their $m$-fold products are executable
all-failure event probabilities. Multilinearity makes their span the
span of all products of $m$ members of $W_A$, which is exactly
\eqref{eq:future-space}. Independence is conditional on $t$; one does
not replace the shared parameter by fresh parameters between trials.

For any fixed future word a deterministic continuation chooses a known
sequence of commands along that word. Its likelihood is a product of
$m$ members of $W_A$, even when the chosen sequence differs on other
branches. Equality of the stated moments therefore implies equality of
all word probabilities. There are finitely many report words. Integrating
an independent continuation seed proves the randomized case. Conversely,
equivalence includes all the spanning all-failure probes; their predictions
determine all the moments in \eqref{eq:future-space}. The constant moment
is one. Only the selected probe is executed and all its $m$ trials are
consumed, regardless of earlier nonfailure reports.
\end{proof}

A fixed positive nonconstant denominator or an arbitrary nonmonomial
factor space is not silently substituted in \eqref{eq:future-space}.
Such models have their own product and test pairings. The theorem below
addresses every sparse monomial space $W_A$, not every finite-dimensional
space of continuous functions.

\subsection{Persistent state and prediction loss}
\begin{definition}[Finite-state filter]\label{def:finite-state}
An $M$-state filter has transitions
\[
 i_{n+1}=U_n(i_n,g_{n+1},x_{n+1}),\qquad i_n\in\{1,\ldots,M\}.
\]
It reads only its previous index, the current command and current report.
Past commands, past reports and retained command-generation or update seeds
are unavailable outside the index. Fresh private coins may be used and
then discarded. At a checkpoint the decoder reads only $i_n$ and a newly
revealed independent query. The known clock permits stage-dependent
transition and output tables. Real command inputs and read-only real
calibration constants are allowed; arithmetic workspace, execution time and
finite-precision implementation of these fixed functions are not the memory
resource of this definition. In particular, this is a finite-state
transducer, not an algorithm that repeatedly rereads an exact prefix.
\end{definition}

For each $1\le n<N$ fix a finite spanning probe list
$H_{n,1},\ldots,H_{n,s_n^{\rm q}}$ as in
Proposition~\ref{prop:tests}, using $m=N-n$ trials. To avoid confusion,
$s_n^{\rm q}$ is the number of queries, not the exact state $s_n$.
After the checkpoint state is formed, choose an independent uniform query
$J$. Announce $a\in[0,1]$, execute that probe using its $m$ fresh trials,
and let $Y$ be its all-failure indicator. All $N$ trials are counted.
The conditional squared-loss excess over the full-history predictor is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:brier}
 \mathcal R(a,p)=\frac1{s_n^{\rm q}}\sum_j(a_j-p_j)^2,
 \qquad p_j=\mu(P_hH_{n,j}).
\end{equation}
This follows by expanding $\E[(a-Y)^2\mid h,J]$; decoder randomization
is averaged on the right. Only one selected checkpoint and one probe are
executed in a run. Simultaneous bounds over checkpoints do not count the
unexecuted probes as physical trials.


\subsection{Exact information and continuous state dimension}
\begin{theorem}[Sparse finite-horizon information law]\label{thm:main}
Suppose the positive finite detector spans $W_A$ and every lookup entry
ranges over $[\eta,1-\eta]$, where $0<\eta<1/2$. For $n,m\ge0$ put
\begin{equation}\label{eq:main-dimension}
 d_A(n,m)=\min\{n(r-1),h_A(m)-1\}.
\end{equation}
Two attainable posteriors after $n$ reports have the same law under every
common future-only continuation of length $m$ if and only if their moments
at the exponents in $mA$ agree. The prediction map on all-failure command
histories has generic rank $d_A(n,m)$, and it has a smooth attainable patch
of this dimension with a continuous section to histories. The smallest
Euclidean dimension of a sufficient encoding continuous on each
known-report history stratum is exactly $d_A(n,m)$.

For a total budget $N$, these dimensions can be attained simultaneously
by a causal exact-real state. Its maximal size is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:peak}
 D_A(N)=\max_{0\le n\le N}d_A(n,N-n).
\end{equation}
Only prior moments with exponents in $NA$ are needed for its updates.
\end{theorem}

\section{Attainable transversality and the exact memory profile}\label{sec:rank}
We first prove the positivity statement that prevents a prior-dependent
loss of rank. No density assumption on the prior is needed.

\begin{lemma}[Strict mixed-moment positivity]\label{lem:mixed-moment}
Let $\alpha_1<\cdots<\alpha_s$ and $\beta_1<\cdots<\beta_s$ be
nonnegative real numbers. For every full-support probability on $I$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:mixed-minor}
 \det\bigl[\mu(t^{\alpha_i+\beta_j})\bigr]_{i,j=1}^s>0.
\end{equation}
Consequently a rectangular moment matrix indexed by two increasing finite
sets of distinct nonnegative exponents has rank the smaller cardinality.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
For increasing positive points $t_1<\cdots<t_s$, the generalized
Vandermonde determinant $\det[t_j^{\alpha_i}]$ is positive. Here is a
self-contained sign argument. Put $t_j=e^{x_j}$. A nonzero combination
of $s$ exponentials with distinct ordered exponents has at most $s-1$
distinct zeros: factor out the first exponential, differentiate, and use
Rolle's theorem inductively. Thus the determinant cannot vanish on the
connected region $x_1<\cdots<x_s$. When the $x_j$ coalesce to $x_0$
from ordered nearby points, its first nonzero Taylor term has the sign
of the Wronskian
\[
 e^{x_0\sum_i\alpha_i}\prod_{i<j}(\alpha_j-\alpha_i)
\]
times the ordinary positive Vandermonde in the $x_j$, divided by
positive factorials. Its sign is therefore positive everywhere in that
region. The same holds for the $\beta$ determinant.

Expanding determinants and applying Fubini gives the finite determinant
integration identity
\[
 \det[\mu(t^{\alpha_i+\beta_j})]
 =\frac1{s!}\int_{I^s}
       \det[t_j^{\alpha_i}]\det[t_j^{\beta_i}]
       \prod_{j=1}^s\mu(\dd t_j).
\]
The integrand is nonnegative, by permutation symmetry and continuity at
zero or repeated points. Choose $s$ disjoint ordered subintervals in
$(l,u)\cap(0,\infty)$. Each has positive prior mass by full support;
on their product the integrand is strictly positive. The integral is
strictly positive. Applying this to any square minor proves the last
assertion. This is the classical total-positivity mechanism
\cite{Pinkus}, with its needed form proved explicitly here.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[A monomial tangent inside the attainable cube]\label{lem:binomial-tangent}
For every $n\ge1$, choose distinct, sufficiently small positive numbers
$c_1,\ldots,c_n$. The failure factors
$F_i=(1+c_it^D)/2$ are simultaneously attainable at interior commands.
At this tuple the differential image of the unnormalized product map
$W_A^n\to\spanop\{t^s:s\in nA\}$ is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:tangent}
 T_{A,n}=\spanop\{t^b:b\in B_{A,n}\},
\end{equation}
where
\begin{equation}\label{eq:tangent-support}
 B_{A,n}=\{jD:0\le j\le n\}
 \cup\!\!\bigcup_{a\in A\setminus\{0,D\}}
          \{a+jD:0\le j<n\}.
\end{equation}
In particular $\dim T_{A,n}=n(r-1)+1$, and the product itself belongs to
this tangent space.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Attainability follows by applying the fixed right inverse in
Lemma~\ref{lem:interior} to $c_it^D/2$ and taking all $c_i$ small enough.
The factors and their implementing commands lie in an open subset of
the allowed cube. Constants $2^{-n}$ do not change a differential image,
so omit them in the remaining calculation. Put $z=t^D$,
$P(z)=\prod_i(1+c_iz)$ and $P_i(z)=P(z)/(1+c_iz)$. These $n$
polynomials form a basis of the polynomials in $z$ of degree at most
$n-1$. Indeed evaluation at $z=-1/c_j$ makes all $P_i$ except $P_j$
zero, and $P_j(-1/c_j)\ne0$.

The product differential is $\sum_i\dot F_i\prod_{j\ne i}F_j$.
Variations in the constant direction give all polynomials in $z$ through
degree $n-1$; variations in $t^D$ give their multiples by $z$.
Together they give all $z^j$, $0\le j\le n$. Variations in an interior
exponent $a$ give precisely $t^a z^j$, $0\le j<n$. The listed exponent
sets are disjoint: two numbers $a+jD$ and $a'+j'D$ with
$0<a,a'<D$ agree only if $a=a'$ and $j=j'$, and none is a multiple of
$D$. Their total cardinality is $(n+1)+n(r-2)=n(r-1)+1$.
Every summand of the differential has this form, proving equality rather
than only inclusion in \eqref{eq:tangent}. The product is obtained by
varying one factor along itself, so is in the tangent.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Generic normalized pairing rank]\label{thm:rank}
For fixed $n,m\ge1$, the map from $n$ all-failure commands to the moment
vector $(\mu(t^sP_h))_{s\in mA\setminus\{0\}}$ has generic rank
$d_A(n,m)$ in \eqref{eq:main-dimension}. This holds for every full-support
prior, including priors without a density. At an interior attainable tuple
it has a smooth $d_A(n,m)$-dimensional local image and a continuous local
section to histories. A projection onto $d_A(n,m)$ of the moment or
spanning-probe coordinates maps such a section onto an open Euclidean ball.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Let $P=\prod_iF_i$, $Z=\mu P>0$, and include the constant test in the
linear map
\[
 L:T_{A,n}\longrightarrow\R^{h_A(m)},\qquad
 LQ=(\mu(t^sQ))_{s\in mA}.
\]
At the tuple from Lemma~\ref{lem:binomial-tangent},
Lemma~\ref{lem:mixed-moment} gives
\[
 \rank L=\min\{n(r-1)+1,h_A(m)\}.
\]
Let $e_0$ extract the coordinate of exponent zero, and put $p=L(P)/Z$.
The derivative of the normalized prediction map on this tangent is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:normalized-derivative}
 Q\longmapsto Z^{-1}(LQ-p\,e_0(LQ)).
\end{equation}
Now $e_0p=1$ and $p\in L(T_{A,n})$. The linear map
$v\mapsto v-p e_0v$, restricted to $L(T_{A,n})$, has kernel exactly
$\spanop\{p\}$ and image in the zero-constant-coordinate hyperplane.
It loses \emph{exactly} one rank. Deleting that identically zero coordinate
therefore gives rank $\min\{n(r-1),h_A(m)-1\}$.

At every tuple the normalized product map is invariant under independent
rescaling of its $n$ nonzero factors, so its rank is at most $n(r-1)$.
The number of nonconstant tests gives the other upper bound. Thus the
attained rank is globally maximal. The command-to-factor linear map is
surjective at every interior command; the same maximal rank is attained
in command coordinates. Its Jacobian entries are rational functions of
the commands with strictly positive polynomial denominator $Z^2$.
A maximal minor nonzero at the displayed tuple has a nonzero polynomial
numerator. Its zero set is a proper real algebraic set with empty interior
and zero Lebesgue measure. This proves genericity throughout the command
cube, not only along the binomial family.

Choose $d=d_A(n,m)$ independent output coordinates and $d$ command
coordinates at that tuple, and freeze the complementary command
coordinates. The inverse function theorem gives a local inverse for the
selected output projection; shrink to a ball with compact closure.
The full image of this section is a smooth local graph. A spanning list
of probes is an injective linear image of the moment coordinates, so the
same conclusion holds after choosing independent probe coordinates.
All section histories are feasible all-failure histories. Their probability
conditional on each parameter is at least $\eta^n$; no failure evidence
has been dropped.
\end{proof}

Notice that when $d<h_A(m)-1$ the attainable image can be curved.
The proof does not replace it by its affine hull. The lower-bound ball
is in a selected output projection, and its lift is a local graph, not
necessarily a flat ball in the ambient prediction space.

\begin{theorem}[Minimal continuous encoding and causal attainment]\label{thm:causal}
The minimal dimension stated in Theorem~\ref{thm:main} is $d_A(n,m)$.
For a fixed $N$, there are compact exact state sets
$\cS_n\subset\R^{d_A(n,N-n)}$ and continuous-in-command causal updates
attaining this dimension at every $n$. Their state represents the
posterior-dependent future law, not an uncharged past-dependent task.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Compose a sufficient continuous encoding with the section in
Theorem~\ref{thm:rank}. Different points in the projected ball have
different predictions, so this composition is injective. A continuous
injection of an open $d$-ball into $\R^k$, $k<d$, is impossible: embedding
$\R^k$ as a coordinate plane of $\R^d$ would contradict invariance of
domain. The decoder need not be continuous for this argument.

For the global upper bound, normalize each observed factor by its own
prior integral, $\widetilde f_i=f_i/\mu f_i$. Each normalized factor is
in the affine hyperplane $\mu f=1$ of $W_A$, with $r-1$ coordinates.
The chronologically ordered $n$ factors determine the posterior product
and use $n(r-1)$ real coordinates. This representation may distinguish
permutations; sufficiency does not forbid that. Alternatively, retain the
$h_A(m)-1$ nonconstant posterior moments in $mA$.
Proposition~\ref{prop:tests} proves this alternative sufficient on every
history. Use whichever representation is smaller. Both are continuous on
each fixed-report stratum. Finite unions of compact continuous images of
the command cube give compact state sets. The cases $n=0$ or $m=0$
have a single class and zero coordinates.

For causal implementation retain normalized factors while
$n(r-1)\le h_A(N-n)-1$. The left side increases and the right side
nonincreases, so there is at most one changeover. When the inequality first
fails, multiply the retained factors and compute the required moments.
Afterward, if $m=N-n$ and the new report factor is
$f(t)=\sum_{a\in A}f_at^a$, update by
\begin{equation}\label{eq:sparse-update}
 M'_s=\frac{\sum_{a\in A}f_aM_{s+a}}{\sum_{a\in A}f_aM_a},
 \qquad s\in(m-1)A\setminus\{0\},\qquad M_0=1.
\end{equation}
All indices $s+a$ belong to $mA$. The denominator is the report
probability and is at least $\kappa$. At the changeover the integrands
are products of $n$ factor monomials and a remaining-test monomial,
so their exponents are in $nA+(N-n)A=NA$. Normalizing each factor
requires only moments in $A\subset NA$. Thus a finite read-only table
$(\mu(t^s))_{s\in NA}$ suffices for all updates. Formula
\eqref{eq:sparse-update} then gives the exact shrinking state to time $N$.
\end{proof}

For arbitrary nonmonomial spaces $W$, the starting identity remains
\eqref{eq:normalized-derivative}, with the true product tangent and
$W^m$. What is special here is the explicit attainable tangent
\eqref{eq:tangent} and the strictly positive mixed pairing. No dimension
formula depending only on $\dim W$ is inferred outside this class.

\section{Interpolation and the analytic inputs}\label{sec:classical}
The interpolation identities in this section are classical. They fix the
normalization used in the geometric argument; they do not assert
statistical attainment. The independent repository derivation of the
finite spectral comparison is acknowledged in \cite{A1v9note}.

\begin{lemma}[Arbitrary-prefix Hermite data]\label{lem:prefix-hermite}
Let $y_1,\ldots,y_p$ be an ordered multiset of real numbers, and let
$q_y$ be the multiplicity of $y$ in the full multiset. The functionals
\[
 f\longmapsto[y_1,\ldots,y_j]f,\qquad 1\le j\le p,
\]
form a basis of the space generated by
$f\mapsto f^{(k)}(y)$, $0\le k<q_y$. Repetitions need not be adjacent.
For positive nodes, application to $f(z)=t^{Hz}$ gives exactly the
complete blocks $t^{Hy}(\log t)^k$, $0\le k<q_y$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
A prefix divided difference depends only on the Hermite data at that
prefix, so belongs to the stated $p$-dimensional space. On polynomials
$1,z,\ldots,z^{p-1}$ its values vanish in degrees below $j-1$ and
equal one in degree $j-1$. The resulting matrix is triangular with
unit diagonal, so these $p$ functionals are independent. Substitution
of $t^{Hz}$ transforms differentiation at a node into multiplication
by the nonzero factor $H^k$ and the indicated logarithmic function.
This is the Newton--Hermite prefix principle; see
\cite[Proposition 7]{deBoor}. It does not assert attainable posterior rank.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Exterior spectral form of determinant volumes]
\label{prop:exterior-profile}
Fix $q$ and nodes $x_1,\ldots,x_q\in[0,1]$, retaining repetitions.
Let $W=(x_i^{j-1})_{i,j=1}^q$ and let
$\sigma_1\ge\cdots\ge\sigma_q\ge0$ be its singular values. Define
$\mathcal V_\ell$ as the maximal absolute Vandermonde product over
$\ell$ labels, with $\mathcal V_1=1$. Then
\[
 c_q\mathcal V_\ell\le\prod_{j=1}^\ell\sigma_j(W)
                         \le C_q\mathcal V_\ell,
 \qquad 1\le\ell\le q.
\]
The constants are independent of all gaps, and the assertion includes
coincident nodes. Equivalently the middle expression is the operator
norm of $\bigwedge^\ell W$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Use the finite Leja ordering of Lemma~\ref{lem:leja-scales}, and let
$d_j$ be its nonincreasing pivots. Put
$P_{j-1}(z)=\prod_{i<j}(z-x_i)$, and let $T$ have as its $j$th
column the coefficients of $P_{j-1}$ in the power basis. It is upper
triangular with diagonal one. Elementary symmetric polynomial bounds
on nodes in $[0,1]$ bound $T$ uniformly in $q$. Writing $T=I+U$
with $U^q=0$ gives $T^{-1}=\sum_{k=0}^{q-1}(-U)^k$, hence a uniform
bound on its inverse.

Evaluation gives $WT=LD$, $D=\operatorname{diag}(d_j)$. If $s$ is
the number of distinct sites, the active leading $s$-block of $L$ is
lower triangular with diagonal $\pm1$, bounded entries, and bounded
inverse. Replace each inactive column by the corresponding coordinate
vector. The resulting matrix $\widetilde L$ and its inverse are
uniformly bounded: it is block lower triangular, with the active block
and an identity block on its diagonal. Because inactive entries of $D$
are zero, $W=\widetilde LDT^{-1}$ still holds.
Multiplication by these uniformly bounded invertible matrices compares
the ordered singular values of $W$ with the ordered $d_j$, including
zero singular values. Finally Lemma~\ref{lem:leja-scales} gives
\[
 \prod_{j=1}^\ell d_j\le\mathcal V_\ell
                    \le\ell!\prod_{j=1}^\ell d_j.
\]
This proves the proposition without dividing by a vanishing pivot.
The Leja lemma is a finite interpolation calculation proved below before
its use in the statistical theorem, so this comparison introduces no
circular dependence on attainment or memory.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Positivity and global covering}
The next two lemmas are the analytic inputs to the intrinsic theorem.
Their complete proofs are placed here, before the application, while the
special-case arguments using them are collected in the appendices.

\begin{lemma}[Strict confluent mixed pairing]\label{lem:confluent-positive}
Let $\alpha_1<\cdots<\alpha_s$ be nonnegative, and let $\lambda$ run
through distinct nonnegative numbers with multiplicities $q_\lambda$
summing to $s$. Include the columns
$t^\lambda(\log t)^k/k!$, $0\le k<q_\lambda$, in this order within
each increasing $\lambda$ cluster. Assume $q_0=1$ if zero occurs.
The matrix of their integrals against $t^{\alpha_i}\mu(\dd t)$ has
strictly positive determinant. Consequently the corresponding rectangular
pairing has full column rank whenever there are at least as many distinct
row exponents as columns.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Put $t=e^x$. The functions become $e^{\lambda x}x^k/k!$.
A nonzero linear combination with total multiplicity $s$ has at most
$s-1$ distinct zeros. Indeed divide by the first exponential and
differentiate $q_{\lambda_1}$ times. Rolle's theorem reduces a hypothetical
$s$ zeros to at least $s-q_{\lambda_1}$ zeros. The remaining terms are
exponential polynomials with distinct nonzero exponents and the same
remaining polynomial degrees: the operator $(D+\lambda-\lambda_1)^{q_{\lambda_1}}$
is invertible on polynomials of each fixed degree bound. Induction gives
a contradiction. If no remaining term occurs, the original function is
an exponential times a polynomial of degree less than $s$, and the
assertion is immediate.

Thus the evaluation determinant at distinct ordered points never
vanishes. Its sign is positive. One can determine the sign by letting
ordered simple exponents approach the prescribed repeated exponents and
dividing by their positive within-cluster Vandermonde factors. Equivalently,
the Wronskian of the displayed exponential-polynomial basis has sign
\[
 \prod_{\lambda<\lambda'}(\lambda'-\lambda)^{q_\lambda q_{\lambda'}}>0;
\]
the exponential factor and the factorial normalizations are positive.
Nonvanishing fixes this sign throughout the connected ordered-point domain.

Use the determinant integration identity from
Lemma~\ref{lem:mixed-moment}, with one determinant replaced by this
confluent evaluation determinant. Both determinants have the same positive
sign on ordered distinct positive points. The integrand is nonnegative
elsewhere by symmetry and continuity. Separated ordered subintervals have
positive prior mass, so the integral is strictly positive. The logarithmic
functions are bounded at zero under the stated hypotheses, which also
justifies Fubini. Select any $s$ rows for the rectangular assertion.
\end{proof}

A bounded semialgebraic format means a bound on the number of variables,
polynomial relations and their degrees in a defining formula; arbitrary
real coefficients are allowed. Quantified formulas of fixed format can
also be used, since real quantifier elimination gives a bound depending
only on this format. Neither a coefficient norm nor a lower bound on a
nonzero coefficient is part of the definition.

\begin{lemma}[Bounded-format sets in a thin rectangle]\label{lem:tame-rectangle}
Let $S$ be a nonempty compact semialgebraic subset of
$\prod_{i=1}^{q}[-a_i,a_i]$, where $a_1\ge\cdots\ge a_q\ge0$.
Suppose its format is bounded and its dimension is at most $p$, with
$1\le p\le q$. There is a constant $C$ depending only on the format
and $q$ such that, for every $\varepsilon>0$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:tame-cover-count}
 \mathcal N(S,\varepsilon)
 \le C\sum_{j=0}^{p}\varepsilon^{-j}\prod_{i=1}^{j}a_i.
\end{equation}
The empty product is one. Consequently $S$ has a covering by at most
$M$ representatives belonging to $S$, of radius at most
\begin{equation}\label{eq:tame-cover-radius}
 C'\max_{1\le j\le p}
           \left(M^{-1}\prod_{i=1}^{j}a_i\right)^{1/j}.
\end{equation}
If all $a_i$ vanish, one representative suffices with radius zero.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
We use two classical real-geometric facts. First, intersections of a
bounded-format semialgebraic set with arbitrary affine subspaces have a
uniform bound on their number of connected components. The bound is
independent of the coefficients; one formulation is the regularity
statement in \cite[Lemma 2.18]{ZhangKileel}, together with real quantifier
elimination. Second, the real Vitushkin--Ivanov entropy inequality is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:vitushkin-input}
 \mathcal N(S,\varepsilon)\le C_q\sum_{j=0}^q
       \varepsilon^{-j}V_j(S),\qquad
 V_j(S)=c_{q,j}\int b_0(S\cap L)\,\dd L.
\end{equation}
For $j>0$ the integral is over affine subspaces of codimension $j$,
with normalized rotational measure and Lebesgue translation measure;
$V_0(S)=b_0(S)$. This is the classical real inequality in
\cite[Theorem 3.5]{YomdinComte}, also recalled as equations (4)--(5)
in \cite{ComteHalupczok}. No nonarchimedean result is used here.

For $j>p$, almost every such affine section is empty: its translation
parameter lies outside a projection of dimension at most $p<j$.
Thus $V_j(S)=0$. For $1\le j\le p$, write an affine section as
$L=u+E^\perp$, $u\in E$, for a $j$-plane $E$. If it meets $S$,
then $u$ belongs to the projection onto $E$ of the containing rectangle.
The component bound, denoted $B$, therefore gives
\[
 V_j(S)\le c_{q,j}B
      \int_{G(q,j)}\operatorname{vol}_j
       \left(\operatorname{proj}_E\prod_i[-a_i,a_i]\right)\dd E.
\]
This projected rectangle is a zonotope. Its volume is the sum, over
$j$-element subsets of the coordinate directions, of their parallelepiped
volumes. Each corresponding orthogonal-projection minor has absolute
value at most one. Hence its volume is at most
$2^j\binom qj a_1\cdots a_j$. Also $V_0(S)\le B$.
Substitution into \eqref{eq:vitushkin-input} proves
\eqref{eq:tame-cover-count}, including zero-width axes. This argument
explains why the products stop at the dimension of $S$, not at the
number of sides of its ambient rectangle.

To respect the integer budget, let $e$ be the maximum in
\eqref{eq:tame-cover-radius} without its constant. If $a_1>0$, then
$e>0$ and $a_1\cdots a_j\le Me^j$ for every $j\le p$.
Choose $L\ge2$ with $C\sum_{j=1}^p L^{-j}\le1/2$. For
$M\ge2C$, \eqref{eq:tame-cover-count} at radius $Le$ is at most
$C+M/2\le M$. Discard empty balls and replace each center by a point
of $S$ in its ball; the radius at most doubles. For $M<2C$, a single
point of $S$ covers it at radius $2\sqrt q\,a_1$, while
$e\ge a_1/M\ge a_1/(2C)$. Increasing the constant proves the assertion
for these budgets as well. A nearest-representative map, with a fixed
tie convention, is Borel. If $a_1=0$, the set is a singleton.
\end{proof}

\section{From attainable geometry to causal memory}
\label{sec:causal-transfer}
The argument separates a geometric question at one time from the
compatibility of the answers at successive times. The following theorem
makes that separation precise. Its hypotheses concern a set, a measure,
and a family of maps; they do not presuppose a quantization law. In
particular, a small singular value or a differential of maximal rank is
not a substitute for the measure hypothesis.

Fix a finite horizon $N$ and a parameter set $\mathcal K$. For each
known $\lambda\in\mathcal K$, let $S_{n,\lambda}\subset\R^{q_n}$ be
the nonempty compact set of states reachable after $n$ reports, and let
$v_n(h)$ denote the exact state. The initial set is a singleton. At
checkpoint $n$, the vector of conditional query probabilities, weighted
by the square roots of their fixed selection probabilities, is
\[
 p_{n,\lambda}(v)=b_{n,\lambda}+G_{n,\lambda}v.
\]
The query is drawn after the label is formed. Thus squared prediction
regret is the squared Euclidean error in this vector. Assume that $G$
and a left inverse of $G$ have norms at most a common constant. The
hypothesis concerns physical query coordinates, not a whitening by
vanishing singular values.

For $1\le n<N$, fix $1\le p_n\le q_n$ and an ordered scale list
$s_{n,1}(\lambda)\ge\cdots\ge s_{n,q_n}(\lambda)\ge0$. The
following requirements have constants independent of $n$ and $\lambda$.

\smallskip\noindent\textup{(G) Global geometry.}
There are invertible linear maps $T_{n,\lambda}$, with both $T$ and
$T^{-1}$ uniformly bounded, and translations $c_{n,\lambda}$ such that
\[
 \widetilde S_{n,\lambda}=T_{n,\lambda}S_{n,\lambda}-c_{n,\lambda}
 \subset\prod_{i=1}^{q_n}[-B s_{n,i},B s_{n,i}].
\]
These sets have uniformly bounded semialgebraic format and dimension
at most $p_n$. Only the format of each set is bounded; its coefficients
need not depend semialgebraically or continuously on $\lambda$.

\smallskip\noindent\textup{(A) Acquired measure.}
There is one law of command exploration, independent of all coding
randomness. At every checkpoint and for each $\ell\le p_n$ with
$s_{n,\ell}>0$, the first $\ell$ coordinates of
$T_{n,\lambda}v_n-c_{n,\lambda}$ have a law dominating $\beta$
times uniform probability on a translated rectangle with half-widths
$\rho s_{n,1},\ldots,\rho s_{n,\ell}$. Here $\beta,\rho>0$ are
uniform. A subprobability carried by one report word suffices, provided
its probability is included. All these laws are marginals of the same
exploration experiment.

\smallskip\noindent\textup{(C) Causal compatibility.}
For $0\le n<N-1$ and every admitted current command $u$ and report $y$, the exact update
is a map, jointly Borel in its state and input arguments,
\[
 F_{n,u,y}^{\lambda}:S_{n,\lambda}\longrightarrow S_{n+1,\lambda}
\]
with Lipschitz constant at most $L_n$, uniformly in $u,y,\lambda$.
It reads the current state, command and report only. The transformed
coordinates in (G) are not required to have a well-conditioned update.

Write
\begin{equation}\label{eq:transfer-profile}
 Q_n(M,\lambda)=\max_{1\le\ell\le p_n}
       \left(\frac{\prod_{i=1}^{\ell}s_{n,i}(\lambda)}{M}
       \right)^{2/\ell}.
\end{equation}
Zero products contribute zero. The clock and the known parameter are
read-only; the persistent resource is the cardinality of the retained
label, as in Definition~\ref{def:finite-state}.

\begin{theorem}[Attainable-geometry transfer]
\label{thm:causal-transfer}
Under \textup{(G)}, \textup{(A)} and \textup{(C)}, the optimal
checkpoint regret with $M$ labels is comparable to $Q_n(M,\lambda)$,
both under the exploration law and in the worst-history criterion.
The constants are uniform in $\lambda$ and every integer $M\ge1$.

More generally, prescribe integers $M_1,\ldots,M_{N-1}\ge1$.
There is one deterministic causal filter retaining at most $M_n$ labels
at time $n$, for which the exact-state error satisfies
\begin{equation}\label{eq:transfer-recurrence}
 e_n\le C\sum_{j=1}^n
    \left(\prod_{i=j}^{n-1}L_i\right)
                     Q_j(M_j,\lambda)^{1/2}.
\end{equation}
The empty product is one. Consequently, if $\mathfrak R_{\boldsymbol M}$
is the optimal maximum-checkpoint regret with these stage budgets, then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:transfer-causal-law}
 c\max_{1\le n<N}Q_n(M_n,\lambda)
 \le\mathfrak R_{\boldsymbol M}^{\rm av}
 \le\mathfrak R_{\boldsymbol M}^{\rm max}
 \le C_N\max_{1\le n<N}Q_n(M_n,\lambda).
\end{equation}
Here the average criterion is the maximum of the expected checkpoint
losses, and the worst-history criterion is the maximum of their suprema;
one selected checkpoint query is executed in a run.
The lower bound permits private or public coding randomness independent
of the exploration law. The constants in this assertion belong to the
fixed horizon and the stated uniform hypotheses.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Apply Lemma~\ref{lem:tame-rectangle} to $\widetilde S_{n,\lambda}$.
It supplies at most $M$ centers in that set with radius at most
$C Q_n(M,\lambda)^{1/2}$, for the actual integer $M$, including
small budgets and zero-width axes. Pulling the centers back by $T^{-1}$
gives reachable states with the same bound up to a uniform factor.
The bounded norm of $G$ gives the checkpoint upper bound. If all scales
vanish, (G) makes the reachable set a singleton and one center is exact.

For the converse, average a randomized decoder conditional on its label
and any public seed. Squared loss cannot increase. There remain at most
$M$ prediction centers. Apply a bounded left inverse of $G$, then $T$,
to these centers, subtracting the fixed translations. Prediction error
bounds below, up to a uniform factor, the distance in any first
$\ell$ transformed coordinates to the corresponding $M$ centers.
For a rectangle in (A), the fraction covered by $M$ balls of radius $r$
is at most
\[
 C_\ell M r^\ell\left(\rho^\ell
                              \prod_{i=1}^{\ell}s_{n,i}\right)^{-1}.
\]
Take $r=a_\ell(\prod_{i\le\ell}s_{n,i}/M)^{1/\ell}$ with a
sufficiently small positive $a_\ell$. At least half of the dominated
mass lies outside these balls. Its squared error is at least
$\beta r^2/2$, up to the fixed metric factor. Maximizing over the
positive products gives the lower bound. Products that vanish require
no lower estimate. Randomized assignment to centers cannot beat the
nearest-center distance. Conditioning on independent public coding
randomness and integrating proves the same assertion for public seeds.
The history law has not been conditioned on an event of probability one
in place of the original report word.

Choose the preceding reachable codebooks with budgets $M_n$.
Starting from the exact initial state, update the retained representative
by $F_{n,u,y}^{\lambda}$ and quantize in the next codebook, using a
fixed nearest-center tie rule. The candidate state is reachable by (C);
hence it belongs to the quantizer's domain. The resulting maps are
Borel. Only the representative's label is retained. With $e_0=0$,
\[
 e_{n+1}\le L_n e_n+C Q_{n+1}(M_{n+1},\lambda)^{1/2}.
\]
Induction gives \eqref{eq:transfer-recurrence}. The bounded physical
query map and the finite sum give the last inequality in
\eqref{eq:transfer-causal-law}. At a fixed checkpoint, every causal
label is also an encoder of the full prefix with at most $M_n$ labels.
The checkpoint lower bounds therefore hold for every causal filter.
They refer to marginals of one exploration law, so their maximum is a
valid lower bound before taking the infimum over filters. This proves
the first inequality. The average criterion is bounded by the
worst-history criterion, giving the middle inequality.
\end{proof}

The set condition (G) controls every history, whereas (A) only needs
one sufficiently rich positive-probability family of histories. These
are different obligations. Condition (C) prevents a small codebook from
being counted as an algorithm that recovers discarded inputs. The
conclusion is a bounded-factor equivalence of checkpoint and causal
resolution, not an equality of optimal constants or a bound uniform in
an unbounded horizon.

\subsection{Positive updates in remaining-test coordinates}
The update hypothesis can be checked before any interpolation or
quantization is undertaken. It is independent of an ordering of scales.

\begin{lemma}[Remaining-test update]
\label{lem:positive-remaining-update}
Let $X$ be compact, and let
$\psi_n=(1,\psi_{n,1},\ldots,\psi_{n,q_n})$ be bounded continuous
tests, not necessarily linearly independent. Suppose that multiplication
by each next report likelihood $L_{n,u,y}\ge\kappa>0$ maps every
component of $\psi_{n+1}$ into $\operatorname{span}\psi_n$, with
uniformly bounded coefficient representations, Borel in the current
input. For posterior moment
states $v=(\nu\psi_{n,i})_{i\ge1}$, the next moment state is a
rational function of $(1,v)$ alone. It is uniformly Lipschitz on
segments joining reachable states, and a reachable representative is
mapped to a reachable next state. The constant depends on $\kappa$,
the coefficient and test bounds, and the dimensions, but not on linear
independence or a nonzero separation between tests.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Write $L_{n,u,y}=b\psi_n$ and
$L_{n,u,y}\psi_{n+1,i}=a_i\psi_n$ in the given representations.
Bayes' formula gives
\[
 v'_i=\frac{a_i(1,v)^t}{b(1,v)^t}.
\]
A segment joining the moment states of $\nu_0$ and $\nu_1$ is the
moment state of their convex mixture. Its denominator is the integral
of $L_{n,u,y}$ under that mixture and is at least $\kappa$.
Numerators, coefficient vectors and moment coordinates are uniformly
bounded. The quotient rule therefore bounds the derivative on the
segment by a constant of the stated kind. Integration along the
segment gives the Lipschitz estimate. Updating the posterior that
represents a reachable state by the admitted input and report is an
actual extended history, proving reachability. Distinct coefficient
representations give the same integrals on moment states and their
mixture segments; independence of the tests was never used.
\end{proof}

This lemma covers remaining products of arbitrary continuous detector
cells on an arbitrary compact latent space whenever the stated
coefficient bounds hold. The monomial and circular experiments require
additional, and different, proofs of (G) and (A). Neither the lemma nor
Theorem~\ref{thm:causal-transfer} asserts that every positive detector
possesses the same collision scales.

\section{Intrinsic resolution at arbitrary exponent collisions}\label{sec:collision-geometry}

The formal future labels determine a uniformly conditioned Newton flag.
The following acquisition argument verifies its positive mass before
we apply the geometric-to-causal transfer theorem.

\subsection{Calibration chamber and determinant volumes}
Fix $r\ge2$ and a compact subset $K$ of the open chamber
\[
 \{(a_1,\ldots,a_{r-1}):0<a_1<\cdots<a_{r-1}\},
 \qquad a_0=0.
\]
The parameter of the experiment is $t\in[0,1]$, with one fixed
full-support prior $\mu$. The known calibration is $a\in K$.
One fixed matrix $C=(c_{ji})$ of column rank $r$ defines the cells
\[
 k_j^a(t)=\sum_{i=0}^{r-1}c_{ji}t^{a_i},\qquad
 \sum_jk_j^a(t)=1,\qquad k_j^a(t)\ge k_*>0.
\]
The command cube is $[\eta,1-\eta]^J$, $0<\eta<1/2$, as before.
These assumptions are imposed on all of $K$; no regularity or
semialgebraicity of $K$ is required. Compactness inside the chamber
separates the one-step exponents, but \emph{does not} separate their
additive sums. The finitely many report likelihoods have a common
positive lower bound $\kappa$.

Fix $N\ge2$, and choose once and for all
$H\ge\max\{1,N\max_{a\in K}a_{r-1}\}$. For $m\ge1$ write
\[
 \Lambda_m=\{\alpha\in\N_0^r:|\alpha|=m\},\qquad
 \Lambda_m^+=\Lambda_m\setminus\{me_0\},\qquad
 q_m=|\Lambda_m^+|,
 \quad x_\alpha(a)=H^{-1}\sum_i\alpha_i a_i.
\]
Here $\N_0$ includes zero. Formal labels are retained even when their
values coincide. All $x_\alpha$, $\alpha\in\Lambda_m^+$, lie in
$[a_*/H,1]$, where $a_* =\min_{a\in K}a_1>0$.
For $1\le\ell\le q_m$ define
\begin{equation}\label{eq:determinant-volume}
 \mathcal V_{m,\ell}(a)=
 \max_{\substack{J\subset\Lambda_m^+\\|J|=\ell}}
    \prod_{\substack{\alpha,\beta\in J\\\alpha<\beta}}
          |x_\alpha(a)-x_\beta(a)|,
 \qquad \mathcal V_{m,1}=1.
\end{equation}
The ordering inside the product is immaterial. The maximum is zero if
fewer than $\ell$ distinct positive future exponents remain. Put
\begin{equation}\label{eq:intrinsic-profile}
 \begin{split}
 p_{n,m}&=\min\{n(r-1),q_m\},\\
 \Psi_{n,m}(M,a)&=
   \max_{1\le\ell\le p_{n,m}}
        \left(\frac{\mathcal V_{m,\ell}(a)}{M}\right)^{2/\ell},\\
 \Xi_N(M,a)&=\max_{1\le n<N}\Psi_{n,N-n}(M,a).
 \end{split}
\end{equation}
Set $\Psi_{0,m}=\Psi_{n,0}=0$. Changing $H$ to any other fixed
admissible value changes only comparison constants. In particular the
invariant does not depend on a convention for measuring exponents.

Use the failure-factor basis $1/2$ and $1/2+\delta t^{a_i}$,
$1\le i<r$, realized by the fixed right inverse of $C$ for one
sufficiently small $\delta>0$. Its ordered $m$-fold products are the
query menu, sampled uniformly after the memory index is formed. Expansion
in the symbolic variables $X_0,\ldots,X_{r-1}$ gives a fixed matrix
$G_m$ of column rank $|\Lambda_m|$: an invertible linear change of
variables on homogeneous polynomials of degree $m$ is invertible.
Consequently the physical query norm and the norm of the formal raw
moment vector are uniformly equivalent. Coincident exponent values impose
relations on attainable vectors, not a loss of column rank of this
\emph{formal} coefficient matrix.

\subsection{A uniformly conditioned flag}
Order the positive nodes greedily. Choose the first label by a fixed rule;
for example take its least node, breaking ties by a fixed label order.
Having selected $x_1,\ldots,x_{j-1}$, select a remaining label maximizing
\[
 \prod_{i<j}|x-x_i|,
 \qquad d_1=1,\qquad d_j=\prod_{i<j}|x_j-x_i|.
\]
Break every tie by the same label rule. After all distinct values have
been selected, continue through the remaining labels; their $d_j$ are
zero. This is finite Leja ordering. The ordering and its use in Newton
interpolation are classical \cite{Leja,BosEtAl}; the estimates needed
here are proved below, including their zero-pivot form.

\begin{lemma}[Newton scales and maximal determinant volumes]\label{lem:leja-scales}
For every calibration,
$1=d_1\ge d_2\ge\cdots\ge d_{q_m}\ge0$. There is a matrix
$L_a$, whose entries have absolute value at most one, such that
\begin{equation}\label{eq:leja-physical}
 (t^{Hx_i})_{i=1}^{q_m}
     =L_a\operatorname{diag}(d_j)(g_j^a(t))_{j=1}^{q_m},
 \qquad
 g_j^a(t)=[x_1,\ldots,x_j](z\mapsto t^{Hz}).
\end{equation}
Divided differences at repeated nodes have their Hermite values. If
$s$ is the number of distinct positive nodes, the first $s$ rows and
columns of $L_a$ form a lower triangular matrix with diagonal entries
$\pm1$; its inverse has a bound depending only on $q_m$. Thus, for
any vector $v$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:leja-metric}
 c_m\|\operatorname{diag}(d_j)v\|
 \le\|L_a\operatorname{diag}(d_j)v\|
 \le C_m\|\operatorname{diag}(d_j)v\|.
\end{equation}
Moreover, for every $1\le\ell\le q_m$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:leja-volume}
 \prod_{j=1}^{\ell}d_j\ \le\ \mathcal V_{m,\ell}(a)
       \ \le\ \ell!\prod_{j=1}^{\ell}d_j.
\end{equation}
All these assertions hold at exact coincidences.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Every distance is at most one. The maximization defining $d_j$ and the
removal of a candidate imply $d_{j+1}\le d_j$. Put
$P_{j-1}(z)=\prod_{i<j}(z-x_i)$. For $d_j>0$ set
$L_{ij}=P_{j-1}(x_i)/d_j$; for $d_j=0$ set that column to zero.
A previously selected node gives zero when $i<j$, and the greedy choice gives $|L_{ij}|\le1$ on all remaining
nodes. The leading nonzero block is lower triangular with diagonal
$\pm1$. Back substitution bounds its inverse in a fixed dimension.
If $d_j=0$, all remaining node values already occur among the earlier
ones, so $P_{j-1}$ vanishes on every node. Newton's interpolation
identity, evaluated at the nodes, is therefore exactly
\eqref{eq:leja-physical}, also when there are repeats. Only the first
$s$ scaled coordinates can be nonzero, proving \eqref{eq:leja-metric}.

For $\ell\le s$, evaluation of the monic polynomials
$P_0,\ldots,P_{\ell-1}$ on any $\ell$ chosen nodes has determinant
equal to their Vandermonde determinant. Its matrix is the selected rows
of $L_{:,1:\ell}$ times $\operatorname{diag}(d_1,\ldots,d_\ell)$.
The Leibniz formula bounds the first determinant by $\ell!$.
For the first $\ell$ selected nodes its absolute value is one, giving
both bounds in \eqref{eq:leja-volume}. For $\ell>s$, both sides
vanish. No division by a zero pivot has been made.
\end{proof}

The next assertion is the attainable part of the argument. Bounds for a
Vandermonde matrix alone would not prove it.

\begin{lemma}[Uniform attainable Newton flags]\label{lem:newton-attainment}
For every $n,m\ge1$ with $n+m\le N$, let
$z_a(h)=(\E[g_j^a(t)\mid h])_{j=1}^{q_m}$ and
$p=p_{n,m}$. At one common interior all-failure command tuple, the first
$p$ coordinates of $z_a$ have a surjective differential. Its least row
singular value is bounded below uniformly over $a\in K$ and every
ordering of the formal nodes, including limiting orderings at collisions.
Under independent uniform acquisition commands, their all-failure
subprobability law dominates $\beta$ times uniform probability on a
translated $p$-cube of side $2\rho$, where $\beta,\rho>0$ are uniform.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
We first justify continuity at all repeated-node configurations, without
an assumption on how a collision is approached. The Hermite--Genocchi
formula \cite{deBoor} expresses $g_j^a(t)$ as the integral of
$(H\log t)^{j-1}t^{Hz}$ over the simplex of convex combinations of
$x_1,\ldots,x_j$; the simplex has volume $1/(j-1)!$.
Since $Hz\ge a_*>0$,
\[
 |g_j^a(t)|\le
  \frac{H^{j-1}}{(j-1)!}\,t^{a_*}|\log t|^{j-1},
 \qquad 0<t\le1.
\]
The right side is bounded and tends to zero at $t=0$. The same argument
with higher logarithmic powers gives continuity, uniform in $t$, as the
nodes vary in their compact range. Define $g_j^a(0)=0$.

For any initial multiset of $p$ nodes, the $p$ divided-difference
functionals are independent on polynomials of degree at most $p-1$:
their values on $1,z,\ldots,z^{p-1}$ form an upper triangular matrix
with diagonal one. They span the Hermite evaluation functionals at the
distinct nodes, with all derivative orders below the corresponding
multiplicities. Applied to $t^{Hz}$, their span is therefore the complete
exponential-polynomial space
\[
 \spanop\{t^{H\lambda}(\log t)^k:
          \lambda\text{ in the multiset},\ 0\le k<m_\lambda\}.
\]
This argument permits repeated nodes at nonadjacent positions. Adjoin the
constant function. The resulting $p+1$ tests form a complete confluent
Chebyshev space, not a selection of isolated high derivatives.

Choose the common binomial factors
$F_i=(1+c_it^{a_{r-1}})/2$, with distinct sufficiently small positive
$c_i$. Their command coefficients are independent of $a$. By
Lemma~\ref{lem:binomial-tangent} their product tangent has
$n(r-1)+1$ distinct monomials. The mixed and confluent pairing of
Lemmas~\ref{lem:mixed-moment} and \ref{lem:confluent-positive} has
rank $p+1$ against the selected tests, at every node configuration.
In the normalized differential
\[
 Q\longmapsto(\mu F)^{-1}(LQ-v\,e_0LQ),
 \qquad v=LF/\mu F,
\]
the product belongs to the tangent and $e_0v=1$. Thus exactly the
constant direction is lost and the differential onto the other $p$
coordinates is surjective.

For each fixed permutation of formal labels this derivative is continuous
on $K$, including coincidences, by the uniform bounds just proved and
positivity of evidence. There are finitely many permutations. Compactness
therefore supplies a common positive least row singular value. First and
second derivatives in command coordinates are uniformly bounded because
the numerator is a polynomial and the evidence is at least $\kappa^n$.

To record the probability step explicitly, complete the derivative's row
space by orthonormal kernel coordinates. A ball of uniform radius in
command space gives a square map whose derivative differs from its
invertible central derivative by at most half its least singular value.
The contraction proof of the inverse theorem gives a fixed-size product
box in output and kernel coordinates, with its inverse inside the command
cube. The inverse Jacobian determinant is bounded below by the reciprocal
of a uniform power of the forward derivative bound. The command density
is constant, and the joint density of those commands and the all-failure
word is at least that density times $\eta^n$. Integrating over the
kernel box proves the stated subprobability minorization. No prior
density is involved and no failure evidence is discarded.
\end{proof}


\subsection{Verification of the transfer hypotheses}
\label{sec:transfer-application}
We now apply Theorem~\ref{thm:causal-transfer} directly. The two
classification theorems in Appendix~\ref{app:complete-routes} give
complete alternative proofs; neither is used in the following argument.

\begin{proof}[Proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:resolution-main}]
At checkpoint $(n,m)$ use the nonconstant formal raw moment vector
\[
 v(h)=\bigl(\E[t^{Hx_i}\mid h]\bigr)_{i=1}^{q_m}.
\]
Let $S_{n,a}$ be its reachable image over all commands and report words.
It is compact. Expansion of the physical product queries gives an
affine map $b+Gv$ with uniformly bounded $G$ and a left inverse, by
the full-column-rank formal product matrix established above. Repeated
exponents give equal coordinates of $v$, not a change in this formal
coefficient matrix.

We verify (G) without inverting a vanishing Newton pivot. Write
$s$ for the number of distinct positive future nodes and decompose the
first $s$ columns of $L_a$ from Lemma~\ref{lem:leja-scales} as
$\binom{A_a}{B_a}$, where $A_a$ is the leading $s\times s$ block.
Both $A_a^{-1}$ and $B_a$ are uniformly bounded. In the Leja ordering set
\[
 T_a=\begin{pmatrix}
       A_a^{-1}&0\\-B_aA_a^{-1}&I_{q_m-s}
     \end{pmatrix},\qquad
 T_a^{-1}=\begin{pmatrix}A_a&0\\B_a&I_{q_m-s}\end{pmatrix}.
\]
When $s=q_m$ the empty blocks are omitted. Both matrices have uniformly
bounded norms. Since $v=L_aD_az$, $D_a=\operatorname{diag}(d_j)$,
we have $T_av=D_az$, including all zero coordinates after $s$.
The associated label permutation is orthogonal and can be absorbed in
$T_a$. Thus an invertible change on the \emph{ambient} raw space exists
even when the reachable set lies in a proper subspace. The sup-norm
bounds for $g_j^a$ in Lemma~\ref{lem:newton-attainment} put this image
in a rectangle of half-widths $B d_1,\ldots,B d_{q_m}$.

For each fixed calibration and report word, every raw moment has the
form $N_j(\boldsymbol u)/Z(\boldsymbol u)$, where $N_j,Z$ are
polynomials of bounded degree and $Z\ge\kappa^n$. Quantifier
elimination applied to this graph bounds the semialgebraic format of
its image independently of its real coefficients. A finite union over
report words preserves a uniform bound. Dividing each factor by its
positive integral against $\mu$ puts its coefficients in an affine
hyperplane of dimension $r-1$; this division changes neither the
posterior nor the normalized moment map. The dimension is consequently
at most $n(r-1)$ and at most $q_m$, hence at most $p_{n,m}$.
Application of $T_a$ does not alter this format conclusion: for a fixed
$a$, its entries are additional real coefficients. No semialgebraic
dependence of $a$, $\mu$ or divided differences on calibration is
required. This proves (G).

For (A), take the common experiment of independent uniform commands and
all its actual reports. Lemma~\ref{lem:newton-attainment} gives an
all-failure subprobability dominating a fixed positive multiple of
uniform measure on a translated $p_{n,m}$-cube in the first Newton
coordinates. Project to any $\ell\le p_{n,m}$ with $d_\ell>0$
and multiply by $d_1,\ldots,d_\ell$. This is precisely the required
rectangle in the first coordinates of $T_av$. Its mass constant is
unchanged. The probability of the report word has already been
included. The command and report laws for different $n$ are prefixes
of one experiment, even though different prefixes can be used to
establish different lower bounds.

For (C), retain the constant moment together with the raw vector.
For each next report factor $f(t)=\sum_i f_i t^{a_i}$, multiplication
sends the remaining tests of degree $m-1$ into those of degree $m$:
\[
 f(t)t^{\alpha\cdot a}=\sum_i f_i t^{(\alpha+e_i)\cdot a},
 \qquad |\alpha|=m-1.
\]
The coefficient vectors are uniformly bounded, and every likelihood
is at least $\kappa$. Lemma~\ref{lem:positive-remaining-update}
therefore gives a raw update with a gap-independent Lipschitz bound
and maps every reachable representative to an actually reachable next
state. No value of $d_j^{-1}$ is used in this recursion.

All the hypotheses of Theorem~\ref{thm:causal-transfer} now hold with
$s_{n,j}=d_j$ and $p_n=p_{n,m}$. Equation~\eqref{eq:leja-volume}
identifies its profile $Q_n$ with $\Psi_{n,m}$, within constants
depending only on the fixed dimensions. Equal stage budgets give
$\Xi_N$. The physical query norm converts the state bounds to the
stated squared prediction regrets. The theorem supplies the common-law
randomized lower bound and one stage-compatible deterministic upper
filter for every integer budget. All constants used above are uniform
on $K$, including collision configurations, which completes the proof.
\end{proof}

Unequal stage budgets are covered by the same verification: replacing
$M$ by $M_n$ at checkpoint $n$ gives the exact error propagation in
\eqref{eq:transfer-recurrence} and the sharp fixed-horizon maximum law
$\max_n\Psi_{n,N-n}(M_n,a)$. No separate acquisition assumption or
re-examination of an exact prefix is needed. The following consequences
return to a common budget at every checkpoint.


\begin{corollary}[Intrinsic bit law]\label{cor:intrinsic-bits}
Let $B_*(\varepsilon,a)$ be the least integer persistent bit budget
for maximum-history streaming regret at most $\varepsilon$ in this
experiment. Uniformly for $0<\varepsilon\le1$ and $a\in K$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:intrinsic-bits}
 B_*(\varepsilon,a)=
 \max_{\substack{1\le n<N\\1\le\ell\le p_{n,N-n}}}
 \left[\frac{\ell}{2}\log_2\frac1\varepsilon+
       \log_2\mathcal V_{N-n,\ell}(a)\right]_++O(1).
\end{equation}
A zero determinant volume contributes zero to this maximum, by interpreting
its logarithm as $-\infty$ before taking the positive part. The same law
holds for the unconditional maximum-checkpoint criterion. At each fixed
calibration the small-error slope is $D_A(N)/2$.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
With $M=2^B$, solving each inequality
$(\mathcal V_{m,\ell}/M)^{2/\ell}\le\varepsilon$ gives the displayed
lower bound on $B$. Constants in Theorem~\ref{thm:resolution-main} change
it by at most a bounded amount, since $\ell$ is bounded. Integer rounding
costs at most one further bit. For fixed $a$, the largest nonzero index
at checkpoint $(n,m)$ is
$\min\{n(r-1),|mA|-1\}=d_A(n,m)$, proving the slope assertion.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Collision orders and a finite tree formula}
The determinant form has a consequence not requiring interpolation
coordinates: it classifies arbitrary orders of tangency to a collision.
It also gives a finite optimization rule for all resolution phases.

\begin{theorem}[Collision-tree determination of memory phases]\label{thm:collision-tree}
Let $a(\theta)\in K$, $0\le\theta\le\theta_0$, be a known path.
For a fixed $m$, identify formal sums that are identical along the path.
Suppose that for every remaining pair of labels
\[
 |x_\alpha(\theta)-x_\beta(\theta)|
       \asymp\theta^{w_{\alpha\beta}},\qquad
 w_{\alpha\beta}\in[0,\infty),\quad 0<\theta\le\theta_0.
\]
After decreasing $\theta_0$ this hypothesis holds for every real analytic
path whose nonidentical sums are analytic at zero. Define
\begin{equation}\label{eq:tree-energy}
 \beta_{m,\ell}=
   \min_{\substack{J\text{ a set of remaining labels}\\|J|=\ell}}
         \sum_{\{\alpha,\beta\}\subset J}w_{\alpha\beta},
 \qquad \beta_{m,1}=0.
\end{equation}
Then $\mathcal V_{m,\ell}(a(\theta))\asymp
\theta^{\beta_{m,\ell}}$. For unavailable cardinalities the volume is
zero. Consequently the checkpoint law is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:tree-law}
 \overline R_{M;n,m}^{a(\theta)}\asymp
 \max_{\ell\le p_{n,m}}
       \theta^{2\beta_{m,\ell}/\ell}M^{-2/\ell},
\end{equation}
with zero-volume terms omitted; the streaming law is its maximum over
checkpoints. The exponents $\beta_{m,\ell}$ depend only on the pairwise
collision orders, and can be computed by a finite tree allocation rule.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Each fixed subset product has order equal to the sum of its pair orders.
There are finitely many subsets, so their maximum has the smallest such
order. This proves the determinant assertion and then
\eqref{eq:tree-law} by Theorem~\ref{thm:resolution-main}.
Constants may depend on the path's
nonzero leading coefficients; the intrinsic determinant theorem, in
contrast, is already uniform on $K$.

For completeness we give the allocation rule and its justification. The
triangle inequality for node differences implies
\[
 w_{\alpha\gamma}\ge
       \min\{w_{\alpha\beta},w_{\beta\gamma}\}.
\]
Indeed a smaller order on the left would contradict the upper bound by
the sum of the two differences on the right as $\theta\downarrow0$.
Thus thresholds on $w$ define nested equivalence classes. Make a rooted
tree from these classes, with root height zero; an internal cluster $C$
has height $h_C=\min_{\alpha\ne\beta\in C}w_{\alpha\beta}$,
and its children are the equivalence classes for orders strictly greater
than $h_C$. Split the root at orders greater than zero. Singleton
children are leaves. Redundant equal-height vertices can be omitted.
For an internal nonroot vertex put
$\Delta_C=h_C-h_{\operatorname{parent}(C)}$. The sum of these increments
on the common ancestor chain of two leaves is exactly their pair order.
Hence, for a selected leaf set $J$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:tree-decomposition}
 \sum_{\{\alpha,\beta\}\subset J}w_{\alpha\beta}
   =\sum_{C\text{ internal},\ C\ne\mathrm{root}}
               \Delta_C\binom{|J\cap C|}{2}.
\end{equation}
Set $F_C(0)=F_C(1)=0$ for a leaf and $F_C(k)=+\infty$ for $k>1$.
For an internal vertex, including the root with $\Delta_{\rm root}=0$,
compute
\begin{equation}\label{eq:tree-dp}
 F_C(k)=\Delta_C\binom{k}{2}+
   \min_{\substack{\sum_{D\text{ child of }C}k_D=k}}
                 \sum_{D\text{ child of }C}F_D(k_D).
\end{equation}
Equation~\eqref{eq:tree-decomposition} proves this recurrence by induction.
It gives $F_{\rm root}(\ell)=\beta_{m,\ell}$. Successive finite
min-convolutions compute the table; no optimization over measures,
posterior encoders or codebooks is involved. For analytic paths the pair
orders are the finite orders of their first nonzero Taylor coefficients,
after identical sums have been identified. This proves the assertion.
\end{proof}

For the affine family of Section~\ref{sec:confluence}, different limiting
clusters have pair order zero, and distinct members of a cluster have pair
order one. Thus a choice of $k$ nodes from that cluster costs
$\binom{k}{2}$; its successive costs are $0,1,\ldots,s-1$.
Minimizing their sum picks the smallest costs from all clusters. Therefore
$\beta_{m,\ell}=\nu_{m,1}+\cdots+\nu_{m,\ell}$. This recovers
Theorem~\ref{thm:attainable-checkpoint}, while its separate proof remains
available. Higher orders of contact between distinct branches require the
pair orders above and are not encoded by the affine multiplicity list.

\subsection{Intersecting collision loci and arbitrary tangency order}
Here is a two-parameter consequence with different ranks on intersecting
loci and arbitrarily deep resolution phases along tangent approaches.
It illustrates the geometry of the determinant theorem rather than
requiring a separate asymptotic analysis for each path.

\begin{corollary}[A two-parameter collision arrangement]\label{cor:two-parameter}
Let $A_{u,v}=\{0,1,2+u,3+v\}$, with
$|u|,|v|\le1/16$, and take the four cells
\[
 k_0(t)=\tfrac14+\tfrac1{16}(t+t^{2+u}+t^{3+v}),\qquad
 k_i(t)=\tfrac14-\tfrac1{16}t^{a_i},\quad 1\le i\le3.
\]
Use any fixed admitted command cube and a fixed full-support prior.
For total horizon five set
$\rho=\max\{|u|,|v|\}$ and
$\tau=\min\{|u|,|v-u|,|v-2u|\}$. Then, uniformly on the square,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:two-parameter-law}
 \mathfrak R_{M,5}^{u,v}\asymp
 \max\left\{M^{-1/3},\ \rho^{1/2}M^{-1/4},\
           (\rho^2\tau)^{2/9}M^{-2/9}\right\}
\end{equation}
for either streaming criterion. The exact peak dimension is nine off the
three collision lines, eight on any such line away from their intersection,
and six at $(0,0)$.

Along $u=\theta$, $v=\theta+\theta^k$ with an integer $k\ge2$,
the three regimes have respective orders
\[
 M^{-1/3},\qquad \theta^{1/2}M^{-1/4},\qquad
       \theta^{(4+2k)/9}M^{-2/9}.
\]
Their crossover budgets have orders $\theta^{-6}$ and
$\theta^{-(8k-2)}$, with regrets of orders $\theta^2$ and
$\theta^{2k}$, respectively. These are comparison orders, not exact
integer thresholds.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
The cells sum to one, lie between $3/16$ and $7/16$, and their coefficient
matrix has rank four: its last three rows separate the three nonconstant
coefficients and the sum of its rows gives the constant. They therefore
satisfy the compact-chamber hypotheses.

At checkpoint $n=3,m=2$ the nine positive formal exponents are
\[
 1,\quad 2,\ 2+u,\quad 3+u,\ 3+v,\quad
 4+2u,\ 4+v,\quad 5+u+v,\quad 6+2v.
\]
They form six uniformly separated groups, with three within-group gaps
$|u|,|v-u|,|v-2u|$. Denote these gaps in decreasing order by
$g_1\ge g_2\ge g_3$. For $\ell\le6$ the determinant volumes
are comparable to one. For $\ell=6+j$, $1\le j\le3$, they are
comparable to $g_1\cdots g_j$: choose one exponent from every group
